# Examdesk UI/UX audit

Date: 2026-11-19. Reference: [ui-ux-learning-guide.md](../ui-ux-learning-guide.md), read completely, including appended section 39. The user instruction selects this guide despite its opening supersession notice. No network sources, npm dependencies or question-content changes were introduced.

The numbered checklist preserves source wording and section/line references. It covers principles, every Nielsen heuristic, common mistakes, alternatives, code/example blocks, historical “In our app” examples, the 20 general questions and all 10 screenshot critiques. Compound source bullets remain one unit with all clauses in scope. Definitions/glossary/bibliography introduce no additional UI obligations. Conditional or mutually exclusive patterns are retained with N.A. decisions, rather than implementing a map, font picker or every library.

Read scope: README.md, every source module/style, all JSON scalar fields (121 questions, 69 coverage entries, three papers, verification files) and the three unused SVG/raster assets. SHA-256 checks after verification confirm the question banks, supporting data and every src/lib module are unchanged. Only verification.json records a new verification timestamp/report.

Method: source heuristic review, WCAG luminance calculations, actual-component React server rendering and local HTTP checks. PASS is limited to that evidence; it is not browser certification, a user study or an accessibility conformance claim. “Before” source line numbers refer to the original files as read; “After” references are current. Any applicable failing state makes the checklist row FAIL. All code failures F01–F14 have implementations; P01–P03 remain external validation with explicit reasons.

Screen key: H=Home (empty/populated progress, topics, weakest topics); L=Learn (list/search/no matches, note page, section links, worked answers); P=Practice (filters/list, blanks/repeated blanks/output, marked report, overrides, retry, run with my answers); M=Mock (start, five questions, timer/pace/low time/expiry, confirm/cancel, report/missing results); S=Scratchpad (ready/empty code/running, compile error, runtime stdout/stderr/failure/timeout, GCC missing, HTTP/static-preview failure, stale output). E=empty, X=error/partial failure, W=loading/busy. ALL includes every one of these states plus both themes, narrow widths, keyboard/focus, screen-reader semantics and colour contrast wherever meaningful. A component-scoped row applies to every state of that component; other screens are N.A. because that workflow is absent.

## Numbered checklist and audit

502 source units: 207 FAIL, 177 PASS, 118 N.A.. Counts describe findings, not a usability score.

| **001.** **Scale.** Bigger reads first. NN/g advises using no more than three sizes (small, medium, large), with the most important element biggest and at most two large elements. | 1. Visual hierarchy and the squint test; [source line 53](../ui-ux-learning-guide.md#L53) | ALL | FAIL | Tiny text, many raw sizes/gaps/radii and an unloaded Inter declaration. Before src/index.css:1,3,8,10,14. | F01: Use an intentional installed UI family; four rem text sizes, weights 400/600, unitless leading, 65ch prose, a 4px spacing scale and three radii. | Resolved in code — `src/index.css:11`, `src/index.css:258`. Browser-dependent validation remains P01. |
| **002.** **Contrast, not hue.** "It's not the actual color of an element that creates the hierarchy" but its contrast with its surroundings. Keep warm saturated colours for warnings. | 1. Visual hierarchy and the squint test; [source line 54](../ui-ux-learning-guide.md#L54) | ALL | FAIL | Two large dashboard slogans, caps eyebrows, saturated hero, symbol tiles, boxed metrics and nested report cards. | **006.** **Reading order.** Users start top and leading (left, in left-to-right languages). Apple: "Order content by relative importance" and "People assume that aligned items are related" ([HIG layout](https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/layout)). The F-shaped scan is "a rough, general shape", not a law ([NN/g](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/f-shaped-pattern-reading-web-content-discovered/)). | 1. | **009.** **Alternatives and when to use which.** Refactoring UI's approach (a design book by Adam Wathan and Steve Schoger) is to de-emphasise the secondary rather than inflate the primary: lower the contrast of supporting text, keep one strong weight. Use size for page-level hierarchy, weight and colour inside components. | 1. | **012.** Proximity can "overpower competing visual cues such as similarity of color or shape" ([NN/g proximity](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/gestalt-proximity/)): two different shapes that sit close still read as a group. | 2. | **013.** Whitespace is the main tool. A label should sit closer to its own field than to the previous field. Headings sit closer to the text below than above. | 2. Respect user text size: use `rem` for font sizes so browser settings scale the UI. | 3. | **031.** **Scale.** Use a few fixed steps, named by role. Material 3 names 15 roles (display, headline, title, body, label, each large, medium, small) from 11 to 57 sp ([Android docs](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/material-components/material-components-android/master/docs/theming/Typography.md)); Carbon's productive set uses steps from 12 px built by a formula ([Carbon](https://carbondesignsystem.com/elements/typography/overview/)); GOV.UK has seven sizes with different mobile and desktop values ([GOV.UK](https://design-system.service.gov.uk/styles/type-scale/)). A product UI needs about four to six. | 4. | **033.** **Variable fonts** pack many weights (and widths, optical sizes) into one file, controlled by axes such as `wght`, `wdth`, `opsz` ([web.dev](https://web.dev/articles/variable-fonts), [MDN guide](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/CSS_fonts/Variable_fonts_guide)). One file per family is usually smaller than four static weights. | 4. System font stack is explicitly permitted and avoids a new dependency/download. |
| **034.** **Numerals.** Tabular figures give every digit the same width, so columns and live counters do not jiggle; proportional figures look better in prose. CSS: `font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums` ([MDN](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/font-variant-numeric)). Some fonts are tabular by default (I measured IBM Plex Sans and Source Sans 3); others need the `tnum` feature (Inter, Manrope, Geist, Atkinson). | 4. | **035.** **Loading.** `font-display: swap`, subset by `unicode-range` so only needed glyphs download, preload the critical file ([web.dev font best practices](https://web.dev/articles/font-best-practices)). `font-size-adjust` normalises x-height when fonts swap or when users choose a font ([MDN](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/font-size-adjust); Baseline since July 2024). | 4. | **036.** **Good versus bad.** (A monospaced code block cannot show this, so here are the numbers.) In Inter's default proportional figures, "1" is 833 units wide and "0" is 1,292 of 2,048 per em (measured), so "1,111 mi" is visibly narrower than "8,888 mi", and a live readout that counts from 8:59 to 9:00 changes width as it ticks. With `tabular-nums` every digit is the same width, columns of miles, times and rates line up on the right edge, and a scrubbed clock stays still. | 4. | **037.** **Common mistakes.** Declaring a font in CSS but never loading it (a silent fallback to the system font); loading four weights as separate files; mixing three families; ignoring fallback metrics so the page jumps when the font arrives; using a font without the glyphs you rely on (arrows, symbols) so the OS fills in from another font. | 4. | **042.** **Lightness does the heavy lifting.** Order and contrast come mostly from lightness; hue adds category. ColorBrewer's guidance for ordered data is light for low values and dark for high ([ColorBrewer](https://colorbrewer2.org/learnmore/schemes_full.html)); Datawrapper: build gradients mainly through lightness and make sure they work in black and white ([Datawrapper](https://www.datawrapper.de/blog/colors/)). | 5. | **043.** **Roles, not hues.** Name colours by job (surface, text, muted, border, primary, on-primary, severity-low) so themes can swap values. | **052.** **Contrast ratios (WCAG 2.2).** Text 4.5:1 (3:1 for large text, 18 pt or 14 pt bold); non-text UI (control boundaries, icons, focus rings, meaningful graphics) 3:1 ([1.4.11](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/non-text-contrast.html)). Ratios are thresholds and are not rounded, so 2.999:1 fails. | 6. | **063.** **A base unit.** Material and Carbon use 4 and 8; Atlassian tokens are multiples of an 8 px base (`space.100` = 8 px, with 2, 4, 6, 12 px steps) ([Atlassian](https://atlassian.design/foundations/spacing)); GOV.UK uses 5 px ([GOV.UK](https://design-system.service.gov.uk/styles/spacing/)). Carbon's scale is 2, 4, 8, 12, 16, 24, 32, 40, 48, 64, 80, 96, 160 px ([Carbon spacing](https://carbondesignsystem.com/elements/spacing/overview/)). The unit matters less than having a unit. | 7. Browser-dependent validation remains P01. |
| **064.** **The 8-point grid.** Use multiples of 8 for sizes, padding and margins, and a 4-point baseline for text and icons; frame icons in a fixed box ([Spec.fm](https://spec.fm/specifics/8-pt-grid)). | 7. | **065.** **Column grids.** Carbon's 2x grid uses 8 px as the mini unit, with 4, 8 and 16 columns from 320, 672, 1056, 1312 and 1584 px breakpoints, 16 px padding, optional gutters ([Carbon 2x grid](https://carbondesignsystem.com/elements/2x-grid/overview/)). Apps with fixed panels (a sidebar plus a map) usually use CSS Grid with fixed tracks and one `minmax(0, 1fr)`, not a column grid. | 7. | **083.** **Not purely better.** NN/g found that for people with normal vision, light mode (dark text on light) usually performed better on legibility tasks, with the advantage growing as text gets smaller; people with cataracts often read better in dark mode. Recommendation: do not make dark the default for a general audience, and let people switch ([NN/g dark mode](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/dark-mode/)). | 9. | **084.** **Follow the system.** Apple: "Avoid offering an app-specific appearance setting" because people may think the app is broken if it ignores their system choice ([HIG dark mode](https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/dark-mode)). The web equivalent is `prefers-color-scheme` ([web.dev](https://web.dev/articles/prefers-color-scheme)) plus the `color-scheme` property so form controls and scrollbars match ([MDN](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/color-scheme)). | 9. | **094.** **Weak signifiers cost time.** In NN/g's eyetracking study of 71 users, pages with weak signifiers took about 22% longer on findability tasks and had about 25% more fixations; ghost buttons were weaker cues than buttons with more visual weight ([NN/g](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/flat-ui-less-attention-cause-uncertainty/)). Flat styling is less harmful with low information density, a traditional layout and high-contrast interactive elements. | 10. | **118.** **Fitts's law.** Time to hit a target is T = a + b·log₂(2D/w), where D is distance and w is target width: bigger and closer is faster ([NN/g](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/fitts-law/)). On a desktop pointer the screen edge stops the cursor, so edge targets are fast; on touch screens edge targets can be slower because users overshoot. NN/g's advice: make targets large, space them out, keep related controls close, make the whole icon-plus-label clickable. | 12. The laws: Fitts, Hick, Miller, Doherty, Jakob; [source line 474](../ui-ux-learning-guide.md#L474) | ALL | FAIL | Narrow CSS hides reset, shrinks nav labels, compresses stats and uses sideways question strips. Targets and touch text are small. Before src/index.css:10,14,19; src/App.tsx:143. | F05: Keep destinations/reset reachable; stack first, expand at content breakpoints, use labelled native pickers, wrap controls/metadata, 44px targets/48px touch and 16px touch inputs. Keep sideways scrolling local to code and add visible action jumps. | Resolved in code — `src/index.css:65`, `src/index.css:275`, `src/index.css:306`, `src/App.tsx:186`. Browser-dependent validation remains P01. |
| **119.** **Hick's law.** Decision time grows with the number and complexity of choices ([Laws of UX](https://lawsofux.com/hicks-law/)). Remedy: fewer, clearer options, grouped; hide rare options (topic 13). It does not say "never more than N choices". | 12. The laws: Fitts, Hick, Miller, Doherty, Jakob; [source line 475](../ui-ux-learning-guide.md#L475) | ALL | PASS | Native labelled controls, ordinary reading order and local task content support this rule; related defects have separate failure rows. `src/App.tsx:122` | Retain supported behaviour; related failures are separate rows. | PASS within source/check scope; browser/user observations are not claimed. |
| **120.** **Miller's law.** People hold only a limited number of items in working memory (the popular "7 plus or minus 2" is a rough, debated figure) ([Laws of UX](https://lawsofux.com/millers-law/)). Remedy: chunk (a phone number as 3-3-4), not an arbitrary cap on menu items. | 12. The laws: Fitts, Hick, Miller, Doherty, Jakob; [source line 476](../ui-ux-learning-guide.md#L476) | ALL | PASS | Native labelled controls, ordinary reading order and local task content support this rule; related defects have separate failure rows. `src/App.tsx:122` | Retain supported behaviour; related failures are separate rows. | PASS within source/check scope; browser/user observations are not claimed. |

| **181.** **When to validate.** Smashing's guide to live validation: late validation (on leaving the field) generally performs better than as-you-type; do not flag empty fields on focus or blur (do it on submit); "reward early, punish late": clear an error as soon as it is fixed, but flag a previously valid field only on blur. As-you-type suits password strength, username availability, character counts ([Smashing](https://www.smashingmagazine.com/2022/09/inline-validation-web-forms-ux/)). | 17. Validation and error messages; [source line 701](../ui-ux-learning-guide.md#L701) | P/M/S/X errors | PASS | Marking occurs on request; no grading/error is applied on first focus/blur or while typing. Original answers are retained. `src/App.tsx:182` | Retain supported behaviour; related failures are separate rows. | PASS within source/check scope; browser/user observations are not claimed. |

| **270.** **One screen, glanceable.** Stephen Few's often-cited definition (in his book _Information Dashboard Design_ and the white papers at [Perceptual Edge](https://www.perceptualedge.com/)) is the most important information, consolidated on a single screen so it can be monitored at a glance ([Few, white paper](https://www.perceptualedge.com/articles/Whitepapers/Dashboard_Design.pdf)); his **bullet graph** replaces the radial gauge with a linear bar against a target ([bullet graph spec](https://www.perceptualedge.com/articles/misc/Bullet_Graph_Design_Spec.pdf)). | 24. | **292.** **Redundant encoding.** Pair colour with shape, text, pattern or position. WCAG 1.4.1 requires it ([W3C](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/use-of-color.html)); Apple: "Offer visual indicators, like distinct shapes or icons, in addition to color" ([HIG](https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/accessibility)); NN/g: colour reinforces shape rather than replacing it. Colour-barrier-free guidance adds line style and hatching, and direct labels instead of colour keys ([Okabe-Ito and J\*FLY](https://jfly.uni-koeln.de/color/)). | 26. | **294.** **Conventions exist, but do not copy blindly.** Carbon's status colours are red danger, orange serious warning, yellow warning, green normal ([Carbon](https://carbondesignsystem.com/data-visualization/color-palettes/)). It is a categorical map, not a monotone severity ramp, and several of its colours are low-contrast. | 26. | **302.** **Figure and ground.** The basemap should recede so the data stands out. Esri's basemap design guide favoured a light grey land and darker grey water for bright thematic symbols; Ordnance Survey advises desaturating or reducing opacity so overlays stand out, and notes vector basemaps let you select only the features you need ([Esri](https://www.esri.com/news/arcuser/0112/less-is-more.html), [OS](https://www.ordnancesurvey.co.uk/blog/effective-basemaps)). Apple offers a "muted" emphasis style for the same reason ([HIG maps](https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/maps)). | 27. | **315.** **One selected, the rest muted.** The selected route is thicker, fully opaque, on top, with a casing (an outline in the map background colour that separates the line from busy roads); alternatives are thinner and in one neutral colour. Navigation SDKs do this by default: Mapbox draws congestion on alternatives only if you enable it (`showsCongestionForAlternativeRoutes` defaults to false) ([Mapbox](https://docs.mapbox.com/ios/navigation/api/2.9.0/Classes/NavigationMapView.html)); TomTom's SDK exposes separate active and alternative colours, widths and outline widths, with opacity from the colour's alpha ([TomTom](https://developer.tomtom.com/assets/downloads/tomtom-sdks/ios/api-reference/0.28.2/TomTomSDKRoutingVisualization/Structs/RouteStyle.html)). | 28. | **327.** **Duration.** NN/g: most UI animation runs 100 to 500 ms; about 100 ms for simple feedback; 200 to 300 ms for substantial changes such as a modal; entering takes a little longer than exiting; "At 500 ms, animations start to feel like a real drag" ([NN/g](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/animation-duration/)). Material's duration tokens run from 50 ms to 1000 ms ([Material docs](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/material-components/material-components-android/master/docs/theming/Motion.md)). | 29. | **328.** **Easing.** Never linear for UI. Ease-out for entering, ease-in for leaving, ease-in-out for moving on screen. | **339.** **Use a slider only when approximate is fine.** NN/g: if the exact value matters, use another control; wide ranges and fine increments make precision hard ([NN/g: slider design](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/gui-slider-controls/)). | **342.** **Keyboard and assistive tech.** The ARIA slider pattern: arrow keys change the value by one step, Home and End jump to the ends, Page Up and Down change by a larger step; `role="slider"` with `aria-valuemin`, `aria-valuemax`, `aria-valuenow` and `aria-valuetext` when numbers are not friendly ([W3C APG](https://www.w3.org/WAI/ARIA/apg/patterns/slider/)). A native `<input type="range">` gives most of this ([MDN](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTML/Element/input/range)). | 30. | **353.** **Grip.** Hoober's 2013 observations of 1,333 people: 49% one-handed, 36% cradling, 15% two-handed; do not assume one hand, and check that fingers do not hide key content; his later reach diagrams were revised, so treat thumb-zone maps as rough ([UXmatters](https://www.uxmatters.com/mt/archives/2013/02/how-do-users-really-hold-mobile-devices.php)). NN/g calls the idea that bottom sheets improve reachability "largely incorrect". | 31. | **354.** **Bottom sheets.** Modal blocks the background; **nonmodal** lets users keep using it (such as panning a map). Use for interruptions or optional branches, not main flows; include a visible Close and do not rely on the grab handle; avoid stacking sheets ([NN/g: bottom sheets](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/bottom-sheet/)). Apple: detents of medium (about half) and large (full); a grabber that also works with VoiceOver; one sheet at a time ([HIG sheets](https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/sheets)). Material: standard sheets have no scrim, a peek height tall enough to signal more content, a half-expanded state at 0.5, max width 640 dp ([Android docs](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/material-components/material-components-android/master/docs/components/BottomSheet.md)). | 31. | **380.** **Native HTML first.** Buttons, links, inputs, `<details>` and `<dialog>` come with keyboard behaviour, roles and focus for free ([MDN: dialog](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTML/Element/dialog)). The ARIA guidance is blunt: "No ARIA is better than Bad ARIA", and "A role is a promise" to add the keyboard behaviour that role implies; ARIA roles do not add keyboard behaviour or styling ([W3C APG: read me first](https://www.w3.org/WAI/ARIA/apg/practices/read-me-first/)). | 33. Keyboard, focus, ARIA and screen readers; [source line 1354](../ui-ux-learning-guide.md#L1354) | ALL | PASS | Native controls, labelled nav/main and a skip link are present; this is source evidence, not screen-reader certification. `src/App.tsx:111` | Retain supported behaviour; related failures are separate rows. | PASS within source/check scope; browser/user observations are not claimed. |
| **381.** **Use the patterns.** The ARIA Authoring Practices Guide documents keyboard models for slider, combobox, dialog, tabs, disclosure and more ([APG](https://www.w3.org/WAI/ARIA/apg/)). Follow them exactly; people expect the standard keys. | 33. Keyboard, focus, ARIA and screen readers; [source line 1355](../ui-ux-learning-guide.md#L1355) | ALL | PASS | Native controls, labelled nav/main and a skip link are present; this is source evidence, not screen-reader certification. `src/App.tsx:111` | Retain supported behaviour; related failures are separate rows. | PASS within source/check scope; browser/user observations are not claimed. |

| **394.** **Layers of tokens.** Primitive (`blue-500: #...`), semantic (`color-action: {blue-500}`), component (`button-bg: {color-action}`). Themes change the semantic layer. | **395.** **Interchange.** The W3C Design Tokens Community Group is drafting a JSON format: tokens have `$value` and `$type` (color, dimension, duration, cubicBezier, fontFamily, fontWeight, ...), groups, and aliases such as `{color.brand}`; it is a community draft, not yet a W3C Standard ([DTCG format draft](https://www.designtokens.org/tr/drafts/format/)). [Style Dictionary](https://styledictionary.com/) transforms tokens into CSS, iOS and Android. | 34. | **397.** **Component API design.** Small, predictable props; variants as a closed set (`variant="primary" &#124; "secondary"`) not free-form class names; compose with children and slots rather than a prop per option; built-in accessibility (names, keyboard, focus); sensible defaults; controlled and uncontrolled modes; no hidden global state. React's own advice, break the UI into a component hierarchy and find the minimal state, applies ([Thinking in React](https://react.dev/learn/thinking-in-react)). | 34. | **405.** **Feedback timing.** 0.1 s feels instant, 1 s keeps flow with a signal, 10 s is the attention limit and needs progress ([NN/g](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/response-times-3-important-limits/)); the Doherty threshold sits near 400 ms ([Laws of UX](https://lawsofux.com/doherty-threshold/)). web.dev's RAIL model turns these into budgets: respond to input in 100 ms, produce frames in 16 ms, load in 1 s ([web.dev: RAIL](https://web.dev/articles/rail)). | 35. This is for qualitative insight, not statistics. | 36. | **449.** **Respond instantly.** Apple's _Designing Fluid Interfaces_ talk opens with response: people are extremely sensitive to latency, and any lag breaks the feeling that the interface is an extension of the hand ([WWDC18 803](https://developer.apple.com/videos/play/wwdc2018/803/), [transcript](https://asciiwwdc.com/2018/sessions/803)). On the web, that means no delay before a press state, and scroll-linked motion driven by the scroll position itself, never by a timer that starts after scrolling. | 39. Fluid motion and scroll feel: what Apple gets right; [source line 1582](../ui-ux-learning-guide.md#L1582) | ALL; native scrolling | PASS | No wheel/touch interception, pinned scene or custom physics exists. Native scrolling retains platform momentum and can always be interrupted. `src/index.css:316` | Retain supported behaviour; related failures are separate rows. | PASS within source/check scope; browser/user observations are not claimed. |
| **450.** **Always interruptible and redirectable.** Every motion can be caught and reversed at any point; the gesture and the thought happen in parallel. Apple's guideline is to let people cancel motion and not make them wait for an animation to finish ([HIG: Motion](https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/motion)). A scroll-driven sequence must run backwards exactly as it ran forwards. | 39. Fluid motion and scroll feel: what Apple gets right; [source line 1583](../ui-ux-learning-guide.md#L1583) | ALL; native scrolling | PASS | No wheel/touch interception, pinned scene or custom physics exists. Native scrolling retains platform momentum and can always be interrupted. `src/index.css:316` | Retain supported behaviour; related failures are separate rows. | PASS within source/check scope; browser/user observations are not claimed. |
| **451.** **Hand velocity over.** When a finger lets go, the content continues at the finger's speed instead of starting from rest. Springs are the only common animation type that can start from any velocity, which is why Apple made them the default ([WWDC23 10158](https://developer.apple.com/videos/play/wwdc2023/10158/)). | 39. Fluid motion and scroll feel: what Apple gets right; [source line 1584](../ui-ux-learning-guide.md#L1584) | ALL; native scrolling | PASS | No wheel/touch interception, pinned scene or custom physics exists. Native scrolling retains platform momentum and can always be interrupted. `src/index.css:316` | Retain supported behaviour; related failures are separate rows. | PASS within source/check scope; browser/user observations are not claimed. |
| **452.** **Project momentum, then snap.** A flick is projected forward with a deceleration rate to find where it would land, and the item settles at the target nearest that projected point (WWDC18). UIKit's scroll deceleration is exponential decay per millisecond: about 0.998 for normal scrolling and 0.99 for "fast", paging-style deceleration ([React Native docs mirroring the UIKit values](https://archive.reactnative.dev/docs/0.26/webview); [worked explanation](https://viblo.asia/p/ios-tim-hieu-ve-co-che-giam-toc-cua-uiscrollview-OeVKBnz2KkW)). | 39. Fluid motion and scroll feel: what Apple gets right; [source line 1585](../ui-ux-learning-guide.md#L1585) | ALL; native scrolling | PASS | No wheel/touch interception, pinned scene or custom physics exists. Native scrolling retains platform momentum and can always be interrupted. `src/index.css:316` | Retain supported behaviour; related failures are separate rows. | PASS within source/check scope; browser/user observations are not claimed. |
| **453.** **Constant friction.** Scrolling feels predictable because its friction never changes, and content slows to an imperceptible stop rather than braking hard (WWDC18). Users carry one mental model of scroll speed across all their apps. Breaking it is what Nielsen Norman Group calls scrolljacking, which hurts control, discoverability, efficiency and task success ([NN/g: Scrolljacking 101](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/scrolljacking-101/)). | 39. Fluid motion and scroll feel: what Apple gets right; [source line 1586](../ui-ux-learning-guide.md#L1586) | ALL; native scrolling | PASS | No wheel/touch interception, pinned scene or custom physics exists. Native scrolling retains platform momentum and can always be interrupted. `src/index.css:316` | Retain supported behaviour; related failures are separate rows. | PASS within source/check scope; browser/user observations are not claimed. |
| **454.** **Rubber-band at edges; don't build a wall.** Past a boundary, content follows the finger with growing resistance and springs back on release, softly signalling the edge (WWDC18). The widely reproduced iOS curve is `offset = (x · d · c) / (d + c · x)` with `c ≈ 0.55`, where `x` is the overscroll distance and `d` the viewport size. It's reverse-engineered, not Apple-published ([Holko](https://www.holko.pl/2014/07/06/inertia-bouncing-rubber-banding-uikit-dynamics/), [Kivy](https://kivy.org/doc/master/api-kivy.effects.dampedscroll.html)). | 39. Fluid motion and scroll feel: what Apple gets right; [source line 1587](../ui-ux-learning-guide.md#L1587) | ALL; native scrolling | PASS | No wheel/touch interception, pinned scene or custom physics exists. Native scrolling retains platform momentum and can always be interrupted. `src/index.css:316` | Retain supported behaviour; related failures are separate rows. | PASS within source/check scope; browser/user observations are not claimed. |
| **455.** **Springs, described by duration and bounce.** Apple now describes every spring with two numbers: a perceptual duration, and a bounce from −1 to 1. Bounce 0 is the general-purpose default (no overshoot). About 0.15 is a small bounce, about 0.3 is clearly bouncy, and values much above 0.4 are rarely right for UI (WWDC23). The physics behind it: mass 1, stiffness `(2π / duration)²`, damping `4π(1 − bounce) / duration`. | 39. | **456.** **Bounce only when momentum earns it.** A tap-driven change uses no overshoot. A fast swipe-to-dismiss gets a little; Apple's Music player uses about 80% damping there. Reward momentum with a small overshoot, but never add bounce to motion the user didn't throw (WWDC18). | 39. | **457.** **Small input, big output; hint in the gesture's direction.** A short flick can travel far. A transition grows from where it started towards where it ends, and things leave by the same path they entered (spatial consistency, WWDC18). iOS waits for about 10 points of movement before deciding a touch is a swipe (hysteresis), so taps aren't mistaken for drags. | 39. | **467.** **Pace budget for pinned or scroll-linked sequences:** about 80–110vh of scroll per beat. The motion completes in the first ~75% of each beat, followed by a ~25% rest so the result can be read. A whole pinned sequence is at most about four viewports. If a sequence needs more, cut beats rather than stretching them. | 39. P0: blocks reliable/accessible use. P1: consequential study friction/accessibility. P2: consistency or external validation.

## Colour contrast from actual CSS

WCAG sRGB calculation: linearise channel c with c/12.92 when c<=0.04045, otherwise ((c+0.055)/1.055)^2.4. L=0.2126R+0.7152G+0.0722B; ratio=(Lmax+0.05)/(Lmin+0.05). Decisions use unrounded ratios; displayed values have four decimals. Text is tested at 4.5:1 even for headings, meaningful UI/focus at 3:1. Decorative separators are not control boundaries. After fixes, text sits on opaque surfaces; the modal backdrop alpha is decorative.

## Real-browser checks still needed (P01)

Automatic approval review rejected Chrome-window access with “Computer Use was not approved for this app.” Browser inventory reported “The browser access host is unavailable.” No browser interaction, screenshots or screen-reader test succeeded. That is why these checks remain open.

1. Visit every coverage-table state at 1440/1024/768/390/320 CSS px in both system themes. Squint/blur 4px and trace the first three fixations: destination, current study action, details. Check long-session note/code readability and low-time labels.
2. Zoom to 200% and apply WCAG text spacing: 1.5 line height, 2em paragraph spacing, .12em letter spacing, .16em word spacing. No clipped headings, timers, summaries, answer controls, diagnostics or dialogs. At 320px the page reflows; only source-code regions scroll sideways. Exercise long 48ch answers.
3. Tab/Shift+Tab from Skip to content through all destinations, pickers, filters, repeated blanks/output, Check, overrides and scratchpad. Enter advances to the correct visible page action. Rings and focused controls must remain visible during local code scrolling; hidden Practice controls must never receive exam focus.
4. Keyboard-open reset and submit dialogs: Keep working gets initial focus, Tab stays contained, Escape cancels, the surviving trigger regains focus. Confirm submission focuses its report and question changes focus the visible question. Use a disposable browser profile; do not clear real study progress during QA.
5. Scratchpad Tab leaves the editor by default; opt-in indentation inserts four spaces, Shift+Tab exits, Escape disables it. Paste multiline code, edit while running, leave/return and verify preserved work, guarded Run, stale labels and recovery.
6. Exercise actual compile failure, stdout, nonzero exit, timeout, GCC absence, stopped server, static preview and the 25s HTTP limit. Verify visible progress/busy, previous-run labels and retained input; a program that never ran must not claim runtime output.
7. Run NVDA (Windows), then VoiceOver/TalkBack on target devices. Check headings/landmarks, links/disclosures, optional field hints, result/read-only blank names, score/override messages, current step and time-band warnings. No second-by-second timer announcements or full diagnostic/report live reading.
8. Test reduced motion, prefers-contrast:more and Windows forced colours. Selected nav/question/step must remain clear through border/weight/semantics. Rings must not be clipped by code overflow or scrolling dialogs.
9. On a real phone test 48px targets/spacing, 16px input text, pinch zoom, keyboard covering actions, safe-area padding and native scroll momentum. No hover-only information should be required.
10. Keep a mock through reload/navigation; reach 15/5/1-minute bands; expire it while in Learn/Practice and follow View report. Check saved deadline/answers against the unchanged behaviour tests.

## Representative-student test protocol (P02)

Recruit five exam-preparing students, separately including novice and experienced C learners. Give neutral tasks: find a weak topic; learn a concept/reveal reasoning; complete two questions and return after filtering; self-mark an equivalent answer; run, diagnose and repair a compiler error; start a mock, navigate, cancel submission, then read its report. Watch without teaching; record success/time/errors/quotes in phone and keyboard sessions. Fix findings and repeat a small round; ask an independent human reviewer to walk the same state inventory. Participants and outreach are external to this code task; no outreach was authorised or attempted.

## Performance measurement protocol (P03)

Profile cold/warm load, note/topic changes, input with a long progress history and the ticking exam on representative low-end/desktop hardware. Record LCP/INP/CLS and long tasks, evaluating p75 rather than an average: LCP<=2.5s, INP<=200ms, CLS<=0.1. The historical routing app’s 60KB map payload and 55fps scrubbing budgets are N.A. to this app. Local loopback assets do not require Internet; stopping the server still needs the documented runner/reload recovery. No service-worker caching or field performance result is claimed.

## Direction and handoff

User/context: a student studying C for long sessions under exam pressure. Direction: calm, legible, direct. Use supplied-guide tool examples as references. Semantic tokens in src/index.css and native shared links/dialogs/code/reports are the source of truth. One lead/support/task hierarchy per screen; four rem type roles, 4px spacing increments, 4/8/12px radii and 100ms ease-out colour feedback. Forbidden defaults: decorative gradients/blobs, lifted/fade-up cards, tiny caps eyebrows, icon tiles, arbitrary downloaded fonts and custom scroll physics. Wrap prose/metadata, preserve source whitespace inside local code overflow. New controls need names, keyboard, focus, contrast and empty/error/busy states. Preserve content/grading, rerun relevant content/behaviour/UI/render/build checks. Code plus this audit is the handoff; a separate Figma system or new npm UI framework is unnecessary.

## Themes & sidebar

2026-11-19: Paper, Sky, Midnight and Ember replace the earlier light/dark palette table above. System is the default (Paper in light mode, Midnight in dark mode), follows OS changes, and resolves in an inline head script before first paint. Theme and desktop collapse preferences use guarded localStorage. Primary colours now carry actions, navigation, focus and progress; accent is limited to highlights. Green/red are reserved for correct/wrong answer states, with words and symbols retained. Light themes have soft shadows and all themes have clear, locally scrollable code blocks. No dependencies, question content or marking changed.

`node scripts/check-ui.mjs` computes and asserts **228 pairs** from the actual CSS, resolving token aliases in all four themes. All text uses the stricter 4.5:1 threshold; control borders/focus use 3:1. Ratios below are the **minimum** for each role across page, surface, surface-2, blank highlight and code background (action labels include default/hover). Decorative separators remain separate from control borders. Light verdicts/Paper accent and Sky/Midnight primaries were tuned slightly to pass on tinted surfaces.

## Colour & delight

2026-11-19: Preserved the Paper, Sky, Midnight and Ember base palettes, System switcher, sidebar collapse/mobile disclosure, focus handling and accessible marking controls. Added eight categorical colour families with three related shades so all 11 topics have their own colour: tinted dashboard cards/accents, topic chips, Learn dots and question stripes. Navigation icons have distinct colours; hero, progress and scores use subtle gradients. The current user request supersedes the earlier audit's prohibition on decorative gradients. Actions retain primary; green/red remain answer verdicts.

Practice Check now gives a 100-particle, 1.2-second canvas burst for a fresh fully correct attempt, an “All N correct!” banner, a score pulse and encouraging partial-result copy, plus wrong-blank shake/✗ and correct-blank pop/✓. Mock submission animates its score ring and celebrates at 80% or above. Viewing reports or self-marking does not create a new burst or streak. The guarded localStorage streak increments on fully correct practice checks, resets on a partial check, and clears with saved progress. Reduced motion uses persistent banners, verdict outlines/icons, a static score border and the actual static score ring; canvas motion is skipped and stops if the preference changes.

Distinct page/section/card/body/meta sizes, generous leading and spacing, and 68ch prose improve reading. Practice and mock questions (including reports) show the optional goal/rules/input-output brief above code, retaining additional prompt information and using sentence-grouped prompt paragraphs when no brief exists. Learn supports escaped, scrollable semantic tables, labelled code fences and Tip/Trap/Note/Remember callouts without dependencies.

Verification: `node scripts/check-ui.mjs` checks 1,240 actual-CSS contrast pairs across all four themes, plus 660 sRGB gradient samples (all text ≥4.5:1; meaningful borders/focus ≥3:1). It also checks topic coverage, brief ordering/fallbacks, escaped callouts/tables, feedback/report states, exact reduced-motion ring values and corrupt/denied streak storage. `npm.cmd run check:behavior`, `node scripts/check-render.mjs` and `npm.cmd run build` pass. ## Colour & delight

Added after the "monochrome and bland" feedback. Base surfaces and text of Paper, Sky, Midnight and Ember are unchanged; each theme gains an 11-hue family (`--hue-*` ink and `--tint-*` surface tokens) that is separate from marking colours (green and red stay reserved for correct and wrong).

- **Topic colour.** Every topic maps to a hue in `src/topicColors.ts`; `data-tone` drives topic cards (tinted background, left accent, gradient progress bar), topic chips, Learn sidebar dots, Learn table headers and the practice/mock question header stripe. Nav icons have distinct hues. The hero, score and feedback elements use tasteful gradients between tint tokens.
- **"What this program does" card** (`src/components/QuestionBrief.tsx`) renders above the code in practice and mock views: goal as lead sentence, bulleted Rules, sample input/output blocks, and the original prompt under "Question details" when it adds information. Without `brief` it falls back to the prompt, split into short paragraphs at sentence boundaries (max about 68ch).
- **Learn notes** (`Markdown` in `Learn.tsx`): styled tables (scoped headers, scrollable region), Tip/Trap/Note/Remember callouts with icons and colours, labelled code blocks, clearer heading spacing.
- **Feedback.** Full marks: a 100-particle canvas confetti (1.2 s, theme colours, `pointer-events:none`, `aria-hidden`) plus an "All N correct!" banner. Partial: encouraging line ("7/10 - fix the red ones") and a score pulse. Wrong blanks get a red outline, a cross icon and a short shake; correct blanks a green outline, tick and soft pop. Mock submit draws an animated score ring and fires confetti at 80% or more. A practice streak (consecutive fully-correct questions) is stored in localStorage inside try/catch and validated on load.
- **Reduced motion.** Confetti is hidden, shake/pop/pulse/ring transitions are disabled; the ring shows its final value statically and the pulse becomes a solid border. Forced-colours styles are provided.
- **Contrast gate.** `scripts/check-ui.mjs` now asserts AA (4.5:1 text, 3:1 non-text) for every `--hue-*` against all base surfaces and tints, text/muted/primary/correct/wrong on every tint, control border and focus on tints, plus sampled gradient interpolations, in all four themes (1240 pairs). It also covers briefs, callouts/tables, feedback states, score rings and streak storage. The script resolves React with an upper-case drive letter, which avoids an "Invalid hook call" caused by two React copies when run from a lower-case Windows shell path.
- Not verifiable without a real browser: animation feel, confetti appearance and visual spacing.


## Mobile navigation pass (2026-11-21)

Guide rules applied: section 14 (peer destinations as a bottom tab bar, show where you are, hamburger only for secondary items), section 31 (48 px targets, safe areas, dvh, nonmodal-free bottom sheets with a visible Close, keyboard-aware docks), section 13 (progressive disclosure: brief, filters and "On this page" fold on phones, open by default only where needed), section 12 (Fitts: primary actions in thumb reach), section 32/33 (focus not obscured via scroll-padding, aria-current, native dialog sheets, no hover-only affordances).

Fixes: the phone top bar is now sticky and part of normal page flow (the old fixed-height shell could be scrolled out of view by scrollIntoView or the on-screen keyboard); the saved desktop collapse state never applies below 48rem. Bottom tab bar (Home, Learn, Practice, Mock) with a Menu sheet for subject, tools, saved progress and theme. Learn: sticky note bar, notes sheet with search, collapsible "On this page", end-of-note "Practise this topic" plus previous/next with note titles, back-to-top, "Back to question" when the reader came from Practice. Practice: filters fold, sticky "Question X of N" bar with previous/next and a question sheet, collapsible brief, "Review concept" link, sticky Check/Next dock that steps aside while the keyboard is open. Mock: sticky question tabs with a compact clock, docked Previous/Next/Submit. Scroll resets to the top on view change and is restored on Back or when returning to Practice.

Still needs a real phone: on-screen keyboard behaviour on iOS Safari, momentum scrolling and screen-reader passes.
